If you believe surviving means living on canned goods and stale crackers, you're stupider than Mike Pozzo. And trust me, that's quite an achievement. That damn freeloader has more talent for emptying the pantry than filling it, but at least he knows a good steak when he sees one. I, on the other hand, hunt. Rabbits, squirrels, birds... whatever you can find. Fresh meat is a luxury around here, but if you want to learn, I can teach you. No waste, no unnecessary noise, and above all no mistakes. I'll give you some simple tasks: setting traps, collecting bait, figuring out where the animals pass. If you do things right, maybe I'll teach you how to use a crossbow, without poking your eye out.     IGUI_SFQuest_Questyno_GeorgeScott_Name = "George Scott",

    IGUI_SFQuest_Questyno_GeorgeScott_Intro_Title = "George Scott 10830,9068,0",
    IGUI_SFQuest_Questyno_GeorgeScott_Intro_Text = "Meet George Scott [Weeklies]",
    IGUI_SFQuest_Questyno_GeorgeScott_Intro_Lore = "George needs me to undertake journeys to recover objects that were used before this disaster. He thinks they can lift our spirits in difficult times. I think I'll give him a hand, he's always waiting for me at the Muldraugh farm",
    IGUI_SFQuest_Questyno_GeorgeScott_Intro_Complete_Text = "You know, I don't blame Nigel's gangs like others do. People do what they can to survive. We're all in the same hell, just with different ways of dealing with it. I'm sure many of those poor guys are walking around with unloaded weapons, bluffing to look more dangerous than they really are. Yet, they are still a problem� a growing problem, like everything else. That's why we need to keep expanding. Staying still means rotting, letting ourselves be consumed.     IGUI_SFQuest_Questyno_JeffreyLewis_Intro_Complete_Text = "Oh, finally someone to talk to. You know, I've always believed that words have enormous power, but not in the way you think. Here, in the apocalypse, it's not about stories or poems, but about how words help you survive. The mind is what keeps us alive, if we don't fall into the design of despair. I'm not one to get lost in chatter, I understand that the world is different now. The things that were important before seem insignificant today. But, you see, I need something: there is a library around, books that could help us understand the past. I like writings, but not only those that speak of poetry. I am interested in manuals, diaries, stories of people who lived before us. Every piece of knowledge we recover is a step towards our salvation, even if it seems that every day takes us further away from hope. And it would be great to find books that are more recent than the 1920s, as rare as they may seem, these texts could tell us about a world we have lost, something that gives us a more complete view of the past. Do you have the courage to collect those pages, or are you more interested in surviving without ever looking back?",

That old river wolf not only fishes enough to feed us all, but he also gave us a roof over our heads when our group took root here in West Point. Without him, who knows where we would have ended up. Now, I do my part, of course. If there are supplies to find, I put my belly—ehm, I mean, my face in it. But food is always scarce and I'm not exactly the type who can resist a good meal. So, if you find something edible around… well, let's say you could earn my eternal gratitude. And maybe even a nice hot dish, if I'm in the mood. Ah, and a piece of advice: if you cross paths with Sam's wife, Pamela, try to talk as little as possible. Don't misunderstand me, he loves her, but let's just say… well, he's a man of another time.
